<td valign="top"><strong>Question:</strong> Which biological function best describes this protein?<br><br><strong>Choices:</strong><br><br><strong>A.</strong> It inhibits retinal guanylyl cyclases at low calcium and stimulates them when calcium is elevated.<br><br><strong>B.</strong> It activates retinal phosphodiesterases at low calcium and suppresses them when calcium is elevated.<br><br><strong>C.</strong> It serves as a calcium-independent structural component of photoreceptor cilia.<br><br><strong>D.</strong> It stimulates retinal guanylyl cyclases at low calcium and inhibits them when calcium is elevated.<br><br><strong>Flash-Next response:</strong><br><br><br><br>1. reasoning: Analyzing the protein sequence reveals several key features:<br>- N-terminal myristoylation signal (MG at positions 1-2), characteristic of photoreceptor calcium-binding proteins<br>- Length of ~170 amino acids, consistent with a small EF-hand protein<br>- Multiple clusters of acidic residues (E, D) arranged in patterns consistent with three EF-hand calcium-binding domains<br>- Hydrophobic patches interspersed with acidic regions<br><br>These features are hallmarks of Guanylate Cyclase Activating Proteins (GCAPs), which are found in photoreceptor cells. GCAPs regulate phototransduction recovery through calcium-dependent feedback:<br>- At LOW intracellular calcium (which occurs after light exposure activates PDE and closes cGMP-gated channels), GCAPs undergo a conformational change and STIMULATE retinal guanylyl cyclases (RetGC1/2) to increase cGMP production and restore the dark state.<br>- At HIGH intracellular calcium (dark conditions), GCAPs INHIBIT guanylyl cyclases to prevent excessive cGMP production.<br><br>Evaluating the options:<br>- A: Describes the inverse of GCAP function (incorrect)<br>- B: Refers to phosphodiesterase regulation (incorrect mechanism)<br>- C: Structural/cilia component (incorrect function)<br>- D: Correctly describes GCAP function - stimulates guanylyl cyclase at low Ca²⁺, inhibits at high Ca²⁺<br><br>2. answer: D</td>
